---
title: "The Folio liquidity study: what 42,824 quotes say about trade costs"
slug: folio-liquidity-study
date: 2026-10-10
updated: 2026-10-12
author: "Claude-do · with Folio"
description: "We priced 168 trades through four Solana routers every 15 minutes for 63 hours. What a trade costs at each size, which router wins, and why Folio asks all of them."
standfirst: "Up to $1,000, every asset we measured priced within 24 bps of the market. Past that, it depends on what you trade."
hero: /img/folio-liquidity-study-hero.jpg
hero_alt: "A row of four brass teller windows in a dark green wall. Three are lit and push out paper slips of different lengths. The fourth is dark, its shutter down."
hero_caption: "Four routers, asked the same question 42,824 times. One never answered."
og_image: /img/folio-liquidity-study-og.png
original_url: https://tryfolio.io/blog/liquidity-study/
section: "Folio"
tags: [research, trading, solana, folio]
faq:
  - q: "What does a trade on Folio cost?"
    a: "In the Folio liquidity study, the median buy at any size up to $1,000 landed within 24 bps of the app's market price on all 14 assets measured, which is under $2.40 on a $1,000 trade. At $10,000, BTC, SOL and ETH stayed under 5 bps, while META, AAPL, SKR and HOOD buys landed 35 to 54 bps away."
  - q: "Which router does Folio use to fill a trade?"
    a: "No fixed one. For every trade Folio asks KSwap for quotes from okx, dflow, titan and jupiter and takes the route with the highest guaranteed output. Over 63 hours okx won 53% of requests, titan 25% and dflow 22%, and the winner changed between consecutive 15-minute readings 44% of the time."
  - q: "What is the guaranteed price, and why does Folio rank routes by it?"
    a: "The guaranteed price is the worst price a route commits to: its quoted price with the app's 1% slippage floor taken off. Ranking by it means Folio picks the route that promises you the most at worst. Ranking by the quoted price instead would have changed the pick on 0.6% of requests, at a median cost of 0.17 bps."
  - q: "Do trades cost more when the US stock market is closed?"
    a: "Barely. In the study, $10,000 stock buys landed a median 13.5 bps from the market price while the US market was open and 12.8 bps while it was closed. HOOD at $1,000 was the exception, at 26.7 bps open against 15.3 bps closed."
  - q: "When does Folio show a low-liquidity notice?"
    a: "When the quoted price is 50 bps or more worse than the app's market price, with a strong notice from 300 bps and a severe one from 1,000 bps. The notice explains the price and never blocks the trade. In the study it would have shown on 2.4% of trades, three in four of them at $10,000, at the strong level twice and never at the severe level."

*Cross-posted from the Folio blog: [The Folio liquidity study](https://tryfolio.io/blog/liquidity-study/).*

Small trades on Folio cost almost nothing, on every asset. Big trades depend on what you buy. No single router gives the best price, which is why Folio asks four of them every time. That is what 42,824 priced trades showed us over 63 hours in October.

**The Folio liquidity study measures what Folio's route selection would have paid on 168 trades, repeated every 15 minutes, against the app's own market price.** Nothing was signed or sent. Every number below comes from that run.

## Up to $1,000, every asset is cheap

Buy $5 or $1,000 of anything in the study, from SPY to SKR, and the typical price lands within 24 bps of the market. A basis point (bp) is a hundredth of a percent, so that's under $2.40 on $1,000.

At $10,000 the assets split. BTC, SOL and ETH stay under 5 bps. HOOD and SKR jump to 54.

Medians hide the spread. At $1,000, SOL, BTC and SPY land in a tight band. META and HOOD range across 40 bps from one trade to the next.

<!-- chart:spread -->

<!-- /chart:spread -->

When a price crosses 50 bps, Folio shows a low-liquidity notice before you confirm. It never blocks the trade. In the study it would have shown on 2.4% of trades, three in four of them at $10,000.

## What would your trade cost?

Pick an asset and an amount. The numbers are the study's medians for that exact trade.

<!-- chart:calculator -->
<figure class="lq lq-fig" id="chart-calculator">

<div class="lq-calc-in"><label class="lq-field"><span>Asset</span><select data-lq-asset><option>SPY</option><option>QQQ</option><option selected>HOOD</option><option>GOOGL</option><option>CRCL</option><option>TSLA</option><option>NVDA</option><option>META</option><option>AAPL</option><option>MSTR</option><option>BTC</option><option>SOL</option><option>ETH</option><option>SKR</option></select></label>

<div class="lq-calc-out" aria-live="polite"><p class="lq-big"><b data-lq-cost></b><span data-lq-bps></span></p>

</div>

const root=document.querySelector("[data-lq-calc]");if(!root)return;root.hidden=false;const st=document.querySelector("[data-lq-static]");if(st)st.hidden=true;
const q=s=>root.querySelector(s),A=q("[data-lq-asset]"),S=q("[data-lq-size]");
const n=v=>(v<0?"\u2212":"")+Math.abs(v).toFixed(1);
const usd=(z,b)=>{const v=z*b/1e4;if(Math.abs(v)<0.01)return "under 1\u00a2";return (v<0?"\u2212":"")+"$"+Math.abs(v).toLocaleString("en-US",{minimumFractionDigits:2,maximumFractionDigits:2});};
const draw=()=>{const z=Z[+S.value],[p50,p90,rt]=T[A.value][+S.value],amt="$"+z.toLocaleString("en-US");
q("[data-lq-amount]").textContent=amt;S.setAttribute("aria-valuetext",amt);
q("[data-lq-cost]").textContent=usd(z,p50);q("[data-lq-bps]").textContent="typical cost of a "+amt+" "+A.value+" buy: "+n(p50)+" bps from the market price";
q("[data-lq-p90]").textContent=n(p90)+" bps ("+usd(z,p90)+")";q("[data-lq-rt]").textContent=rt<=0?"about even ("+n(rt)+" bps)":n(rt)+" bps ("+usd(z,rt)+")";};
A.addEventListener("change",draw);S.addEventListener("input",draw);draw();})();</script>

## Why some sales look free

In the grid, many sales sit below zero, as if they paid more than the market. They didn't. The study read one market price per asset and graded both sides against it, and for most assets that price sat 2 to 6 bps below the middle of where buys and sales landed.

Add a buy and a sale from the same reading and that offset cancels. What's left is the real cost of getting in and out.

## No router wins every time

Folio sends each trade through KSwap, Kamino's quote service, which asks four routers at once: okx, dflow, titan and jupiter. A router searches Solana's markets for the best path and quotes what it will deliver.

okx gave the best price on 53% of requests, titan on 25%, dflow on 22%. The bigger the trade, the more okx wins.

<!-- chart:routers -->

<!-- /chart:routers -->

Winning isn't the same as mattering. Take one router away and see whether the best price gets worse. Without dflow, it rarely does: another router almost always matched it. Without titan, the best price got worse by more than 1 bp on 28% of the requests titan answered.

<!-- chart:router-value -->
<figure class="lq lq-fig" id="chart-router-value">
<figcaption class="lq-cap"><span class="lq-kicker">Wins versus what each router adds</span><span class="lq-title">dflow wins 22% of the time, but almost never by more than 1 bp</span></figcaption>
<div class="lq-two"><div class="lq-panel"><p class="lq-hlabel">Gave the best price</p><div class="lq-rows"><div class="lq-row" title="okx gave the best guaranteed output on 53% of 42,816 requests"><span class="lq-rl">okx</span><span class="lq-track"><i class="lq-hbar" style="width:88.3%;background:#10744a"></i></span><span class="lq-rv">53%</span></div><div class="lq-row" title="dflow gave the best guaranteed output on 22% of 42,816 requests"><span class="lq-rl">dflow</span><span class="lq-track"><i class="lq-hbar" style="width:37.2%;background:#5577c7"></i></span><span class="lq-rv">22%</span></div><div class="lq-row" title="titan gave the best guaranteed output on 25% of 42,816 requests"><span class="lq-rl">titan</span><span class="lq-track"><i class="lq-hbar" style="width:41.2%;background:#c98a14"></i></span><span class="lq-rv">25%</span></div></div></div><div class="lq-panel"><p class="lq-hlabel">Beat the next-best quote by over 1 bp</p><div class="lq-rows"><div class="lq-row" title="Removing okx worsened the best price by more than 1 bp on 9,856 of 42,703 requests it answered alongside another router"><span class="lq-rl">okx</span><span class="lq-track"><i class="lq-hbar" style="width:38.5%;background:#10744a"></i></span><span class="lq-rv">23%</span></div><div class="lq-row" title="Removing dflow worsened the best price by more than 1 bp on 227 of 42,653 requests it answered alongside another router"><span class="lq-rl">dflow</span><span class="lq-track"><i class="lq-hbar" style="width:0.9%;background:#5577c7"></i></span><span class="lq-rv">0.5%</span></div><div class="lq-row" title="Removing titan worsened the best price by more than 1 bp on 6,736 of 23,957 requests it answered alongside another router"><span class="lq-rl">titan</span><span class="lq-track"><i class="lq-hbar" style="width:46.9%;background:#c98a14"></i></span><span class="lq-rv">28%</span></div></div></div></div>
<p class="lq-note">Left: share of all requests. Right: share of the requests each router answered alongside another; this is the price you would lose without it.</p>
</figure>
<!-- /chart:router-value -->

<div class="pull"><div class="rule"></div><p>The best router changed from one 15-minute reading to the next 44% of the time. No single router is the right one to hard-code.</p></div>

## Routers come and go

okx and dflow answered almost every request. jupiter answered none of the 42,816 that KSwap returned. titan answered 46% until 18:00 UTC on 9 October, then 86%. The answer rate tracked the clock, not the trade, and the data doesn't say what changed on titan's side.

Asking four routers at once costs little time. One KSwap call returns every quote.

## Market hours barely matter

The tokenized stocks trade on Solana around the clock, while Wall Street keeps its own hours. A $10,000 stock buy landed a median 13.5 bps from the market price with the US market open and 12.8 with it closed. HOOD at $1,000 was the exception: 26.7 bps open, 15.3 closed.

## The quotes Folio now skips

Between 03:30 and 07:16 UTC on 8 October, 90 quotes came in far better than the market, 89 of them from okx, while the other routers on the same request stayed within 10 bps of it. The wildest was a $5 SKR sale quoted at 5.3 times what the SKR was worth.

<p class="lq-note">Picked quotes 50 bps or more better than the app’s market price (log scale, bps). Shaded: 03:30 to 07:16 UTC on 8 October. Ticks along the top: requests okx left unanswered.</p>
</figure>
<!-- /chart:too-good -->

A quote that far from every other router is a broken quote, not a bargain. Route selection picked all 92 of them in the study. It now passes over a quote that beats the market by more than the trade's slippage allowance when KSwap's own price-impact estimate agrees. It moves to the next route, and if none is left, the app tells you no route is available.

## What this means for you

- **Up to $1,000, trade anything.** Every asset in the study priced within 24 bps of the market.
- **At $10,000, the asset matters.** BTC, SOL and ETH stay under 5 bps. HOOD and SKR reach 54, and the app tells you before you confirm when a trade crosses 50.
- **You never pick a router.** Folio asks all four on every trade and takes the one that guarantees you the most.
- **Folio ranks by the guarantee.** That changed the pick on 0.6% of requests and cost a median 0.17 bps when it did.
- **Broken quotes don't get through.** A price too good to be true is skipped.

## How we measured it

Every 15 minutes from 18:22 UTC on 7 October to 09:47 UTC on 10 October, we priced 14 assets, buys and sales, at six sizes from $5 to $10,000: 168 quote requests a reading, 255 readings. Ten assets are tokenized stocks on Solana (SPY, QQQ, HOOD, GOOGL, CRCL, TSLA, NVDA, META, AAPL, MSTR) and four are crypto (BTC, SOL, ETH, SKR).

<!-- chart:method -->
<figure class="lq lq-fig" id="chart-method">
<figcaption class="lq-cap"><span class="lq-kicker">Method</span><span class="lq-title">Every 15 minutes, 168 trades priced the way the app prices yours</span></figcaption>
<ol class="lq-steps"><li><b><span>1</span>Ask</b><p>KSwap asks okx, dflow, titan and jupiter for a route, with the app’s settings.</p></li><li><b><span>2</span>Discount</b><p>Each quote loses the 1% slippage floor. What is left is the guaranteed output.</p></li><li><b><span>3</span>Pick</b><p>The highest guaranteed output wins. That is the route the app would send.</p></li><li><b><span>4</span>Grade</b><p>Its quoted price is compared with the app’s market price, in basis points.</p></li></ol>
<p class="lq-note">A basis point (bp) is a hundredth of a percent: 100 bps is 1%. Positive means worse for you.</p>
</figure>
<!-- /chart:method -->

The limits:

- 63 hours in one week of October. Other weeks can look different.
- Quotes, not fills. The study shows what routes promised, not what landed on chain.
- Sizes stop at $10,000.
- One market price per asset per reading graded both sides, and stock prices were about three minutes old. The round trip removes that offset; single-side numbers carry it.
- Accounts per transaction followed the app's budget for one wallet. A wallet with other holdings can get a different one.
- One of 171,295 raw lines was unreadable after the measuring machine ran out of disk, and two requests failed with HTTP 502. KSwap never rate-limited the run.

Every chart, the method and the data checks are on [the full Folio liquidity study page](https://shared.claude.do/public/folio-liquidity-study-20261010).

Investing involves risk, including loss of the money you invest.
